ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U49	Let us start a debate. Name to Member Juris Villums.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U50	Very honourable Speaker of the Saeima! Presidium! Head of Government! Ministers! Ladies, thank you very much! Ladies and gentlemen, I will try to briefly explain the substance of this proposal and the related proposals of the next Group of the Association of the Regions of Latvia in this draft law, which largely coincides with our proposed amendments to the future draft law under consideration today - the draft law "Amendments to the Law on Excise Duty". The proposal of the Group of the Regions of Latvia is to supplement the list of vehicles exempt from vehicle operating tax, both because it is not in proportion to vehicle utilisation intensity, and because a number of vehicles in the other Baltic States are not subject to such a separate operating tax. Our proposals also include a compensatory mechanism, which provides for an appropriate excise duty on fuel to be paid for the maintenance and use of roads precisely by those who make more use of road infrastructure in their daily lives. At present, the government and the majority of the commission responsible are in favour of double increases in the tax burden in this area, as the next draft law raises excise duty, whereas this is intended to postpone the planned change in vehicle operating tax arrangements for a year and to raise the tax rates by an additional 7 per cent. In short, the tax burden on the remaining population is still being increased. On 2 December, the Commission for Mandates, Ethics and Applications will see an initiative signed by 10 652 Latvian citizens entitled “On roads without pits”, which provides for a vehicle operating tax to be added to the fuel price. So overall, this is largely in line with these proposals from the Group of the Alliance of the Regions of Latvia. I call on us to support the idea of an initiative signed by more than 10 000 people and the proposals of our group, both in this draft law and in the next one, "Amendments to the Law on Excise Tax". Thank you.

Unfortunately, we are still being specially protected by one group. And no matter how nice it is, that this alcoholic drink - a beer - has enough browning in Latvia, however, only one of each of the two beer bottles sold in Latvia has been produced here. This represents only 16 per cent of the total market for alcohol. The proportion of beer to alcohol is inadequate. This ratio is currently 1 to 3.6. On average, in Europe it is about 1 to 2. And this is my offer to raise a slightly faster rate for beer, so that in the next year's budget we will get an additional 1.8 million, which we could target to support families with children. And one of my proposals, which in the Commission was not supported by this, is to keep reliefs for the spouses who care for a child under three, or for the spouse who cares for three children and one of them is at pre-school age, or... The third group that I propose (I will speak on this proposal yet) is the spouse who takes care of five children. That is, including the retail premium - 20 percent and the wholesale premium - 40 percent VAT... all inclusive in this... this change in the price of the shelf. ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U106	Good morning, honourable colleagues, honourable colleagues! “From the heart to Latvia” is against the increase of excise duty on fuel. The increase in excise duty will increase inflation, because, as is well known, changes in the prices of almost all goods, products and services are directly related to the price of fuel, which is one of the main drivers of inflation and thus affects everyone in Latvia. Once again, it will hurt people with lower incomes, i.e. almost one third of Latvia's population, including more than 40 percent of single parents. And this indicator is, by the way, the third highest in the European Union. It will affect teenagers and older people. The number of adolescents at risk of poverty has increased to 47,7 percent and the number of people over the age of 65 has risen to 39.3 percent. Unfortunately, this dynamic is only moving up and the number of these people is rising. You can't do it in the fields without a car. This is particularly true in areas where there is no public transport or where it is travelling once a day. The machine is especially needed for families with children. If a car is assumed to travel only 50 kilometres each day, family expenditure increases by more than EUR 50 per year. It may be a ridiculous sum for those here, but it will be an enormous burden for hundreds of families. The increase in excise duty will also result in higher costs to cover losses for public transport operators. The increase in the budget from 35.9 million to 36.2 million or it will prove insufficient, especially if fuel price reductions stop and prices start to rise. Moreover, the government does not already rule out this possibility by deciding that, should the planned financial loss to public transport operators prove insufficient, the Ministry of Transport will only have to prepare a request for additional funding from unforeseen circumstances. ‘ From the heart of Latvia” believes that it is unacceptable to increase the rate of excise duty, as lower fuel prices both increase fuel consumption and promote economic activity in general. This is particularly important at a time when the growth rate of the economy has slowed down. According to the information published by the State Revenue Service, during the nine months of this year the excise tax plan as set out in the 2015 budget has already been exceeded by more than 18 million euros. Even if it is assumed that the increase in fuel consumption will be halved in 2016, it is possible to collect an additional EUR 10.3 million of excise duty revenue in the draft budget. ‘ According to Latvia, all factors related to the revenue from excise duty on fuel, including the impact on inflation and fuel consumption, the possible increase in smuggling and in particular the social consequences, have not been taken into account. I consider it unacceptable to take such an important decision only on the basis of accounting calculations as to how much additional tax can be charged in the state budget and what the approximate impact of excise duty on inflation might be. After the figures, we do not see people again and we do not hear the public. How long? It's a game with fire.

ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U159	Let us start a debate. Name of Member Inguna Silver.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U160	Good morning, dear colleagues! ‘ From the heart to Latvia” has prepared proposals for the draft law “Amendments to the Law on Budget and Financial Management”, which would improve the transparency of the budget, ensure that both we and the Members could understand, when deciding on the budget, how the specific expenditure of the state budget will contribute to the achievement of the objectives set in the National Development Plan and other planning documents and what the results will be achieved. I think there would be a story here, a parable of how three friends, the blind, the deaf and the lame, went on their way. Kurlais says, “Hearing says there will be war.” “If you don’t shoot your eye in such a war!” says the blind. ‘ Then there will be a glide!” says the lame. And so three friends went on, speaking of war. Nothing's going on, we're just talking. This similarity can be applied to the draft budget for 2016, to the process of drafting and consulting the draft laws accompanying it and can be fully applied to the current coalition, which neither sees nor hears what is happening around it. It cannot really go ahead - and this year's draft budget shows it - even though it always speaks about how well the country is developing, how good the budget is, how good the taxes are. It would be interesting to know how many of you can now be responsible for increasing or reducing the funding for individual national budgetary programmes. What will be the impact of cutting costs, what will be the benefits for the country of increasing expenditure? I assume that perhaps an official knows this, but not the largest of us, because the explanations in the draft budget that can be found there are, in fact, almost nothing... It is therefore not possible to find information on which budget money is actually being diverted. The re-allocations of appropriations, as you know well, are regularly decided by the Saeima and approved, but it is only known that in a budget programme money has been left out, will not be used and can be re-distributed to other measures. But will such redistribution help to achieve the objectives of the National Development Plan? Will economic breakthrough be achieved? This question remains unanswered. I believe that it is finally time to put an end to this understanding of budgeting and approval and to start a new life. And the first step towards this right path is now to establish that it is compatible with the National Development Plan when drawing up the 2017 budget. It is no less important to ensure that today, when we vote in favour of the 2016 budget, it is already known that we, Members of the Saeima, will not only receive from the budget drawn up such figures, as is actually the case this year, but also an explanation of what policies are planned to be implemented. It is important that every one of us, every businessman, citizen, recipient of benefits and pensions in the country knows to whom and how taxpayers' funds are spent and what will ultimately be the result of all this. Perhaps you believe that it is impossible to implement it, that it is something impossible. I believe that today any businessman who creates his own business plans his own business, knows exactly how he spends money, how he does it. And that is absolutely the opposite of what the state is doing right now. When deciding on his expenses, I think he always has a long and careful thought-out of who will spend the money on it, because he has to make money. In turn, a country of which we are also proving to be a part can afford to spend without thinking about what will be achieved, because this money is not earned by officials. It is provided by entrepreneurs, workers and, I must say, pensioners. Therefore, I invite you to support the proposals from the heart of Latvia today to pave the way for these changes and to make the decisionrs more visible, heard and able to move. I will not say anything new ( Dep. A.Latkovsky: “Right!” ), but the longer the disease is allowed, the harder it is to treat. So don't wait until next year what you can do now. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U190	Dear colleagues, The wisdom of the Latvian people is taught: do what you do and think well (From the hall: “Galu!” ) and make it smart. Unfortunately, as Members of the Saeima, when we look at issues relating to the use of public funds, we find from time to time that no single public authority responsible for the use of taxpayers' money and, above all, rational, effective - I would like to emphasise in particular - legal use, these smart words are not being heard. I would like to remind you only of the most important violations found during the checks carried out by the State Audit Office. When examining the financial statements for 2014, the payment of illegal advances has been established at the Ministry of Culture for a total amount of 223 thousand euros. The National Health Service had already partially paid for services to be received in 2015 for almost 230 thousand euros. The prison administration paid an advance of EUR 126 000 for services that will only be received in 2015, and it could continue this list with other amounts. What is common to cases of unlawful irrational handling of funds? It is a wish to transfer funds at the end of the year in advance for next year's payments. However, the Law on Budget and Financial Management establishes that such advance payments are unlawful. But how do authorities react? They are trying to explain that this is not a deplorable act, but an effective use of funds. Unfortunately, the Prime Minister has also publicly expressed his view that illegal advance payments are not a condemnation because they have been made in good faith. Perhaps someone can answer the question of how it is possible to act in good faith and at the same time illegally? Personally, it would be interesting for me to hear what, for example, the State Revenue Service would say to entrepreneurs, who would also, if they were not willing to pay a higher corporate income tax, make some payments for services received in the next or perhaps even further period already in this particular year, explaining that in this way they are also effectively dealing with funds and reducing taxes payable to the State. For they have also done in good faith, and in this way it is not reprehensible. Probably we expect from the State Revenue Service in relation to the businessman a completely different scenario. This would certainly not be seen as an act of good faith on the part of economic operators. But what do these advances prove? They reflect poor budgetary planning, problems with the organisation and execution of procurement. Because it turns out that, at the end of the year, the institution has these free cash reserves and can pay for the goods and services received next year already in the year. I think it would be logical for the institution to pay for these services at the moment and in the year it actually receives these services, but this year it reduces expenditure by saving resources and, in fact, by rationally acting in relation to the total cash flow of the country, these payments are made next year. Therefore, I believe that our proposal to reduce by 25 per cent the part of expenditure which has been unduly paid in the form of advance payments would only promote a fair treatment of taxpayers' money, so the managers of disciplined institutions would be rationally and, above all, legitimately treated with taxpayers' money. I therefore call for support for our proposals to promote the responsibility of these authorities for the legal and effective use of public funds. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U234	Dear colleagues, We work with the draft law “Amendments to the Value Added Tax Act”. The Commission has received 29 proposals. ( The head of the sitting was knocked down with a hammer.) 1. - That's Mr. Tutin's proposal. There is no support in the Commission.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U235	Let us start a debate. The name of Member Julia Stepanenko.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U236	Dear colleagues, To understand the substance of this proposal, I will explain my vision and that of the political group on the implementation of this VAT Directive in relation to VAT... I am sorry, in relation to the VAT treatment of housing administration services. Fully contrary to the principle of good governance... It is very unpleasant to identify these fundamentally unscrupulous practices of the application of the directive, which were already begun at the time of Brussels's 'best students'. A year has passed since the elections. Is that the time for unpopular decisions? Because when was it really known about this infringement procedure? Already at the beginning of 2014. But was it time for unpopular decisions? I don't think so. Despite the directive's good intention - to regulate taxation in the Member States, to improve the quality of life and the efficiency of administration - it is to be concluded that we often find that the directive, regardless of its translation, is being abused. In this case, without going into full detail of the substance of the directive, the public budget will be successfully tied up with additional measures. Latvia has not taken care to lay down a transitional provision for the application of VAT for management services or has exceptionally requested that it be exempt from VAT. The Ministry simply wrote a letter a year ago that citizens' solvency prevented VAT from being introduced for administration services. Regrettably, the excuse: "We are being fined by the directive, otherwise we will be fined!" has become so common that it does not require any further discussion. And it is already quite normal practice for the Ministry to have the sole right to interpret the directives and the legislative changes based on directives are not discussed with industry. In this regard, I would like to understand exactly what the responsible ministry has done to clarify the necessary steps to be taken and to understand what is required of Latvia. Perhaps it is time to give up stereotypes about the rigidness of directives, use our critical thinking and start to defend the interests of our country once, by participating actively in discussions in good time. Why is there no exception in the VAT Directive that applies to Latvia? Does this not mean total powerlessness of Latvian officials? I would remind you, colleagues, that Latvia still has the possibility to apply two reduced rates, while some other countries have even a third, even less than five percent, i.e. the VAT rate of two percent. And Latvia is currently applying only one reduced VAT rate. That's 12 percent. This reduced rate of 12 per cent also applies to hotel services, which are essentially similar to management services. Many industry experts have learned about this innovation at a late stage and havetened to submit their proposals and proposals. However, the discussion did not take place and they remained unheard. Now I can tell you all that there are serious concerns about the correct application of VAT, there is a risk of double taxation and a risk of distorting the management services market. There will be an additional burden on municipalities, as the amount of housing benefits will increase, not to mention the dramatic situation of consumers' solvency. Latvians are strongly attached to their real estate because they have fallen into the trap of bank loans, many have no choice but to devote their salaries to most payments related to real estate. I therefore call on you to support this proposal, which is intended to bring the issue of VAT into line with this decision which has been implemented quickly. Thank you.

The law on the administration of residential houses, which refers only to general terms and which is understood by the cost of the administration of residential houses, also does not provide an answer. This law will have to be applied throughout the territory of Latvia, and it must be clearly recognised that the understanding of what services will be subject to value added tax will definitely differ. This could lead to unequal treatment of citizens of our country as well as their cash instruments, because only from the master's skill or misunderstanding and fear of the interpretation applied by the State Revenue Service will depend on how much our citizens will pay more. I would also like to draw attention to the fact that this tax will not apply to those who live in private homes but will apply to those who live in apartment homes and whose wallet is not particularly thick. Therefore, with this proposal, I propose that the Cabinet of Ministers should provide, as far as possible, an explanation of which management services are subject to value added tax. And the Cabinet of Ministers should ensure that explanatory information on the services subject to this tax is available. In my opinion, such a solution would ensure a common approach not only by the managers, but also by the State Revenue Service and would only indicate a normal state attitude towards its citizens. Please support my proposal. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U332	Colleagues, I will immediately talk about a number of my proposals that are linked. Further three and also transitional provisions. Because in these proposals, which are now proposals 3, 4 and 5, perhaps a bit embarrassing is a rate that is significantly higher than it is today. But this is due to the fact that my proposal is to increase the rate of slots and gaming tables by about 10 percent each year. Unfortunately, it must be said that it has now been historically established that this sector is particularly spared, even though the budgetary needs are much higher than we are in this sector, so we should also eat and spend accordingly. I will give you a few figures, although I understand that this will not change anything in substance. It will also be accepted as a favor for those who do not want to tax gambling... Even gambling. The key is not to get out of the head of those who have big money and who can do it in public space. So how has the net turnover in total million euro changed over two years? I will call nine months because it is comparable to nine months this year. So in 2013 there were 124 million, and in 2015 there were nine months - 157 million. Number of machines, number of machines. In 2013 it was 8200, and now it is over 8600. Expected profit and loss. I call on my colleagues here to listen. In the nine months of 2013, it was 23.8 million. In the nine months of 2015, the expected profit is 39.3 million. The increase is about 80 percent. Profitability... the expected profitability or return on turnover. The increase is from 19.12 percent to almost 25 percent. Now imagine - a business with a 25 per cent profitability, and every year profitability increases by 3 per cent. Therefore, my proposal is to increase this tax by 10 per cent, given that, for example, there has been no increase in the tax rate since 2012, while inflation has been common, it has been above 10 per cent, and the increase in the average wage over this period has exceeded 16 per cent. ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U377	Dear Members, Honourable Members! Proposal 5 concerns the differentiated non-applicable minimum. There is a new concept that our government and our tax system introduce in terms of income... taxes withheld from the income of a natural person. And it is in this bill that this is obviously a natural place. At the same time, we would like to add that, despite the fact that this new, rather revolutionary idea has been supported by the government and repeatedly, or repeatedly, supported by the Budget and Financial (Tax) Commission, this proposal has not been supported by either employers or workers. The differentiated non-taxable minimum will affect all workers whose monthly salary does not exceed EUR 1 500, but does not apply at all to those with higher incomes. And that's the main thing... the main claim, the main objection, why we cannot support this idea, this concept and these proposals, which include both this viewable one and the others that will be ahead of it. In our view, however, it should be borne in mind that the progressive tax that we are going to be talking about today is a very fruitful idea that applies in all the Member States of the European Union, with the exception of the three Baltic States, plus Hungary, Slovakia and Bulgaria. Given that it is precisely the income of Latvian taxpayers who receive much more than 1500 euros, it is very important to understand that this right - progressive - tax would not simply be a tax on wealth. The rich people of Latvia use only part of their income for consumption, and only from this part pays value added tax and also excise duty. The rest of the country are forced to shift all their income towards consumption, thus paying consumption taxes on all their income, complementing the state budget. It is therefore the progressive nature of personal income tax that would be a way of reducing inequalities. I would like to add that the unlimited minimum that we are now talking about will only be able to be fully recovered after submission of the annual income statement to the State Revenue Service. So in many cases, when due to the employee's ignorance or lack of skills a declaration will not be submitted to the State Revenue Service, the money will simply remain in the state budget. All these shortcomings in reality make this “good” idea a burden for the taxpayers of Latvia, especially for those with a revenue of less than 1500 euros. However, other proposals and other draft laws are waiting for us to ensure the principle of progressiveity in the implementation of personal income taxes in Latvia, therefore this idea should not be supported. And both on this proposal and on all the others, which are linked to the differentiated non-eligible minimum, the Group will abstain from voting. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U403	12. - Member Pimenova's proposal. Not supported.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U404	Let us start a debate. Name to Member Igor Pimenov.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U405	Colleagues, today we have launched negotiations on the application of the principle of progressiveity to personal income tax in the Republic of Latvia. Today, all political parties are speaking less or more about this principle of progressiveity without exception. Even the Speaker of the Saeima mentioned the progressive tax in his recent holiday address. Social inequality has become challenging in our country, and disproportionately heavy tax burdens are on the least provided people of Latvia. I, as well as my group Members, have put forward proposals for a progressive tax in the last two years, and we have also presented it now. Even more, in 2009, the Group “Sarkanas Centrs” did this for the first time, i.e. when the crisis started in Latvia. In June this year, with the help of the Chairman of the Commission for Economic, Agricultural, Environmental and Regional Policy, Romana Nadiņš, we had organised an expert conference in the Saeima entitled “What progressive tax is needed in Latvia?” , and based on the results of its conference, we have developed a project with the members of the public organisation Progressive, on which I have reported to you today. I have already told you that it is not just a wealth tax. As I have already said, the principle of progressivity imposes a higher tax burden on those who have more opportunities to earn. The owner of the company has much more opportunities to increase income than a paid employee. The businessman also has more opportunities than a paid employee to optimise, i.e. to reduce tax payments, without infringing the rules. We encourage the introduction of progressive taxation of personal income: all types of income are taxed at the same rate - both wages, capital income and capital gains, including dividends. Instead of one existing rate, there are four corresponding taxable income intervals and the rates should be determined on the basis of the annual taxable income. I would not call specific rates and specific tax ranges to simply save time. All of this is described in the tables that you received before the sitting, and I hope that they are now in front of you. It is very important to mention that there are additional benefits, additional effects or wealth effects, as the English say willover effects, which makes the principle of progressivity a strong tool that can both equalise or reduce social inequalities in the country, obtain additional budgetary resources and motivate economic growth in the country. First, the tax burden is shifted to high income, and this will reduce the overall labour tax burden, which in turn reduces the costs for Latvian producers and increases their competitiveness. Secondly, reducing the tax burden increases the real income of workers and thus the demand on the market, which in turn will motivate entrepreneurs. And thirdly, what is very important and topical in our country, especially in our country, is the reduction in costs that will motivate entrepreneurs to come out of the shadow economy. We checked this model using the data that we received from the State Revenue Service, namely data on income in 2014 and taxes paid, in order to check whether there would really be the effect we expected. And our calculations have shown and shown that it is quite achievable. More than that. I would like to emphasise that this model has a neutral fiscal impact. It cannot be said that the application of this regulation or this proposal, if you support it - and I will now call on you to do so - will have the potential to damage the budget or lead to additional expenditure. On the contrary. On the contrary! The situation will be similar to what is now, if we are already talking about revenue in the budget. This model will offset the overall tax burden that is now payable by both those with lower revenues and those with higher revenues. Especially those who now pay dividends. In any case, this law is also applicable to other, direct taxes, which we will continue to talk about today. The system needs to be improved. Please support my proposal.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U406	Thank you. I closed the debate. Does the rapporteur wish to add something on behalf of the Commission?

ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U486	Let us start a debate. The name of Member Silvia Symph.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U487	Dear colleagues, Dear colleagues, The proposal is to give municipalities the right to decide themselves, taking into account the specific situation in which they are located, on the level of compensation for training costs. At present, the law provides that the maximum compensation for training costs may be 30 per cent. So with this amendment we could remove this restriction. The situation in public administration and municipalities varies considerably. The public administration is mainly concentrated in Riga, where there is the highest average wage in the country and more career opportunities. This is also reflected in the remuneration of public administration employees. In 2014 the average wage in the public administration was 45 percent higher than in municipalities. Moreover, the gap between average wages in public administration and municipalities is only increasing. In 2008, when the law on remuneration had not yet been passed, the difference in average remuneration in public administration and municipalities was only 37 percent, and now it is 45 percent. In a regional context, the average wage varies almost twice. The highest is, of course, Riga, and the lowest is, of course, Latgale. Thus, there are also significantly different possibilities for the employees of the state administration and municipality to cover the cost of the training fees, which, take into account, is the same for everyone, regardless of what I'm talking about earlier, and regardless of whether the workplace is in Riga, Preili, the Prize or somewhere else in Latgale. In view of this, the argument of the Ministry of Finance that the conditions in the country and in the municipalities regarding compensation for training fees should be the same does not hold criticism. At present, they are not the same. By giving the municipalities the opportunity to decide on the amount of the training costs themselves, we would give them one more instrument to encourage them to improve the qualifications of existing employees or to prepare them for occupations where there is a shortage of staff. For example, this year the Saeima supported amendments to the Barn Courts Law, which significantly increased the educational requirements for the Chair, Vice-Chairman and members of the orphan court from 2021. The current situation is that about 50 per cent of the relevant officials' education is not in line with the law, which means that about 250-300 people will have to obtain the required level of education. The average level of remuneration for these officials is lower than for a simple rapporteur at the Ministry of Finance, but the cost of the training fee will amount to several thousand. Taking all these arguments into account, I ask... I call for my proposal to be supported. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U569	Hello, Madam President. Members! Mrs. Strautums! Colleagues! I have to say that, despite geopolitical peripetations, which test the values, system, strength and ability of political leaders of both Europe and Latvia to make sometimes difficult and responsible decisions, from an economic perspective, you can be as critical as you wish, but I will say that 2015 has been a good year for the Latvian economy as a whole. Latvian economy continues to grow faster than expected by experts. Economic growth of 2.5 percent ranks Latvia among the leaders of the countries of the European Union. And this growth, fellow Members, is mainly ensured by the increase in domestic demand and the fact that the impact of the drop in demand in Russia has decreased. Exports to Russia dropped by 25 percent, but the total volume of exports to us in Latvia is growing. Entrepreneurs have been very large edgers, able to reorient their markets to other countries. Production volumes are increasing. And, as the facts show, the following year will continue to grow. We have learned to work a little more efficiently, with more added value. But, colleagues, there is still much to be done. Firstly, to ensure that in the Latvian economy at least 20 per cent of gross domestic product is made up of industry, production. Now it's nothing 12 percent, for a healthy economy this figure has to be almost doubled. By 2020, colleagues, we must be able to create 28 000 jobs. In the first half of the year, GDP growth was 2.5 percent, employment growth was only 0.1 percent. In the second half of the year it is already better - the outlook for the year is quite good overall. There are two essential blocks to achieve this: one is the energy block, where we have done a great deal this year, I must say. The electricity market is open. For the first time in Latvia's history, the price of electricity for households and producers will fall next year. We have achieved this year's... next year's budget provides for huge funding to compensate for the increase in the mandatory procurement component. The price of electricity will not rise, as it is very important that our producers do not pay more than German, Austrian and Polish producers. Throughout the government, the greatest step has been taken in opening up the gas market since the first attempts to do so in 2002. I hope that the Saeima will also succeed as well. “ Let’s hope! Yes!’ ) Colleagues, and the second block is tax policy. My position is quite firm: the government must keep the promises made in tax policy. Among us, I think, there are some who have created some jobs in their lives and therefore understand this very well: if taxes are not predictable and stable over a long period of time, this creates additional stress for the businessman, breaks down plans. No one needs that. It is very easy to make a decision to stop doing business. It happens about this: one click and make a decision when the patience pot is full. It is very difficult to start something again, and it is difficult to get them back into the economy. In this situation, we see what is happening in Europe, which is happening in Russia. More than ever, our country must support peace as much as possible. And peace is not stagnating. Peace and a healthy spirit in the economy need to be supported. Otherwise, we can forget about creating new jobs, creating added value, conquering export markets. National economic policy must stimulate citizens to earn more, because other money, colleagues, we do not have just the money that we earn - and I stress here the word 'profit'! - businessmen. Colleagues! While the media is dramatic (I will say - really dramatic!) refugee issues, which, however, will have little impact on the Latvian economy in the next 50-100 years (it will be neither negative nor positive; if someone numbers the labour force, it is not the right attitude when we talk about refugees), while we are actually doing one worse thing: we are handing over the tax on micro-enterprises... The idea of micro-enterprises tax, for which I will say I will have a huge impact on the pressure of the Latvian economy right now. Who benefits from it? I think no one really. For my part, I can remind you once again that the figures available to the Ministry of Economic Affairs are clear. Business start-up activity is dangerously falling: every year since 2012 founded by a thousand - a thousand! - the number of undertakings which cease their activities is lower, and there is also a sharp increase in the number of undertakings which cease their activities. In 2012, there were 4442 companies, but in ten months of this year 8672 companies closed their doors. And I will say, colleagues: largely because of the fact that we are discussing what to do with micro-enterprises' tax - there will be no, no, no sectors or no restrictions? - Man doesn't understand what his company will be tomorrow, and he closes the door. Colleagues! There are thousands behind each of them... Not only the businessman himself, but also his family. Thousands of people! Therefore, colleagues, I hope, however, and I believe in your understanding that we will not end up breaking the micro-business tax law. I also call for support for the proposals made by the Ministry of Economic Affairs. We have submitted a proposal to the Saeima to clarify the aim of this law by deleting the reference to sectors with low income potential, because there is no justification for limiting the tax regime if professional professionals and specialists have decided to start their economic activity. In particular, colleagues need to stimulate the creation of companies that will produce high added value products. Secondly. I am glad that the decision on 37 sectors has been postponed...
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U570	Mrs. Revealer! The time allowed for your debate has come to an end... Are there any objections from Members if they combine the two speeches? ( From the hall: “Let it speak! ” ) Thank you.

On the agenda - draft law “Amendments to the Law “On State Social Insurance”, second reading. The draft law has been declared urgent. On behalf of the Budget and Financial (Tax) Commission, Member Imants Debtor.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U712	Colleagues! We are working on draft law No.385/Lp12. The Commission received 11 proposals for a second reading. 1. is a proposal by the Budget and Financial (Tax) Commission itself. Supported by the Commission.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U713	Members are in favour.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U714	2. is the proposal from Member Igora Pimenova. There is no support in the Commission.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U715	Let us start a debate. Name to Member Igor Pimenov.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U716	Hello, dear colleagues! I propose to remove from the draft law “Amendments to the Law “On State Social Insurance” a provision which is in force in the law and which states that the competence of the Cabinet is to determine the maximum amount of the object of compulsory state social insurance contributions. To a large extent, this competence creates the possibility of a draft law and a tax such as solidarity tax. The regulations currently in force in the Cabinet of Ministers set the maximum amount for which this is mentioned and which is equal to 48 600 euros per year. Consequently, 4300 people are exempted from social contributions. As far as I know, this amount will be even higher next year, namely EUR 51 900. I am talking about the ceiling that we are now discussing. As a result, the number of potential beneficiaries of high wages who will not pay this social tax will fall to 3700 people. Consequently, above these ceilings, the overall tax burden on labour on large wages is being reduced and a regressivity, which is not socially fair, is being introduced. A fair and truly appropriate Latvian solidarity pension system would be the abolition of the maximum amount of the object of mandatory contributions. Consequently, all mandatory social payments above the existing ceiling would be included in the social budget and there would be no need for us to create the kind of legal ruins that we have just accepted before lunch break. That is, solidarity tax. I understand that the maximum amount or so-called ceilings for social tax payments has been introduced because, should they be abolished, it would lead to too much public benefit costs, especially those such as the cost of parental benefits. And the country can be understood! But, on the other hand, if we understand where the dog is buried, then it would probably be useful not only to increase this illogicalness in our tax system, but to change the system at its very root, namely to remove the ceiling for the payment that we call social tax, on the one hand, but, on the other hand, to arrange for parental benefits in such a way that the recipients of high wages do not receive such large benefits, which the country would simply not be able to provide, thus running counter to what it has now come into. I think it's a natural and logical solution. I ask you to support this proposal. Thank you.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U717	Thank you. I closed the debate. Does the rapporteur wish to add something on behalf of the Commission?
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U718	Yes, honourable colleagues, Mr. Pimenov has already told us precisely. We have two possible options. Either we abolish the ceiling on contributions or we introduce a solidarity tax. We have already made this choice. I think everyone is... We agree that both things will not be done at the same time. So, on behalf of the Commission, I am calling on Member Pimenova not to support the proposal.

Only in competitions can you assess the level of readiness already achieved to be a champion and look for improvement of methods used in the training process. Therefore, I do not understand what principles the Ministry of Education and Science, under which Muryan Sports Gymnasium is located, plans the budget for 2016 for this institution so that it does not include expenditure on participation in international sports competitions for gymnasium students. The Gymnasium Statute on the stated and stated objectives of the action states - to prepare high-class athletes, candidates for Latvian national teams, start-ups at the Olympics, world, European championships, as well as to promote the achievement of high sporting results in the Olympic sports. It also seems illogical that in 2015 Murjāņi sports Gymnasium started large-scale renovation works of sports manners, internatas, schools and hotel facades in the amount of almost 3 million euros, while participation in sports competitions in 2016 lacked 185 thousand 380 euros. The question arises: are we building after construction, or are we going to achieve higher sports achievements by acquiring the experience of competitions in new, renovated premises, best training and domestic conditions? This exact figure - 185 thousand EUR 380 - is not my figure, but it is based on the estimates of Muryan gymnasium coaches. Thank you to my colleagues who supported further progress on this proposal. In the hope that in the future, the Ministry of Education and Science will include the competition expenditure in the budget package, please vote in favour of this proposal by Saeima Members, thus ensuring our new athletes' participation in sports competitions in 2016. Thank you.

It still needs to be allocated to healthcare in order to ensure that healthcare workers' salaries increase not by 5 but by 10 percent. And 10 million will, however, be devoted to reducing payments for patients and paying more services, as well as reducing queues. I would like to remind you that the government's health promises made in the declaration have not been met and, contrary to the government's declaration, expenditure on health care as a percentage of gross domestic product has not increased but has fallen even further: from 3 percent to 2.9 percent. And this is far from the 4.5 percent recommended by the World Health Organization. As a result, as I said before, and as I have already talked about and talked about, when discussing the draft laws under consideration at first reading, 14 percent of the population cannot afford healthcare in Latvia because of their cost – as evidenced by the medical trade union. I would like to say that there are very many important conditions for quality health care - hospitals, medicines and modern facilities - but with all these prerequisites health care will remain limp and will not provide quality if there are no specialists who will be able to apply the facilities that will be able to prescribe the right medicines. The level of pay for our certified sisters is very low. There is a danger that we could lose this subsector. And it would be even more a blow to health care like that. I very much ask you to take these threats seriously and to use every opportunity to prevent them and to provide quality health care not only for those who are now in the hall, but for all the other inhabitants of Latvia. In Latvia work has started on developing a new standard of basic education based on competences. This standard provides for the teaching of informatics from first class onwards. In order to ensure effective improvement of the learning process in line with current trends in Europe, it is necessary to equip the IT office with an interactive board. With its help, each student will have the opportunity to participate in various practical tasks, to acquire technical skills to work with an interactive table. This will allow both the teacher and the pupils to perform their work more quickly and more easily. Teachers need to have access to the Internet to quickly inform their parents about their school achievements, delays, homework, and behaviour at school through the e-class programme. To make efficient and easy use of the virtual environment and speed up computer operation in computer science hours, it is necessary to update the program with Windows 10. I ask the Parliament to financially support the modernisation of Liepaja City Secondary School of Informatics and to find an opportunity to allocate 5 thousand euros for this goal. ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U1465	Let us start a debate. The name is Mr. Wallary Ageshin.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U1466	Dear colleagues, Liepaja Karosta, on the coast of the Baltic Sea, has a unique military complex, which is special in the history and architecture of Latvia and the world. The port covers about a third of the city’s total area, located in its northern part, and has about seven thousand inhabitants. According to the Liepaja City Development Plan 2014-2020, the Atmood Boulevard is one of the central streets of Karosta, which connects part of the city with Karosta. The Karosta today is also a very popular tourism object, which is visited annually by a large number of tourists. Tourists and visitors to the North Moore, one of the symbols of the Karosta - the St. Nikolay Sea Cathedral of Liepaja, as well as the Latvian and global tourism facilities - the Karosta prison. From the inhabitants of Karosta, as well as guests of the city, the municipality regularly receives criticism of the unsatisfactory state of the Awakening Boulevard, which is particularly dangerous for pedestrians and cyclists. The street has not been repaired for several decades, and now it is the only major unconstructed street in the city. In order to carry out the reconstruction project of the Atmogas Boulevard, Liepaja City Council has carried out a technical assessment and identified the preliminary costs of the project, which amounts to 1 million EUR 560 thousand. The possibility of attracting European Union structural funds to implement the project was also examined; unfortunately, it had to be concluded that such a solution does not exist, because this street is not defined as a transit street. It should also be taken into account that, according to the development plan of the territory, the implementation of the National Armed Forces Development Programme is foreseen in that territory. The investment programme approved by Liepaja City Council for 2015-2020 emphasizes the above priority because reconstruction of the Awakening Boulevard would provide additional opportunities. First, expand the infrastructure of the National Armed Forces and Naval Forces in line with the Development Concept of the Ministry of Defence. There are such strategically important objects as NBS Marine Force Training Centre, NBS Naval Force Military Base, Baltic Waterway Training Centre. Second, ensure safe movement of the people of Karosta to work and training facilities. And thirdly - encourage aesthetically attractive and safe travel for tourists and city guests to the favourite tourist attractions. The implementation of the project is planned to be divided into layers. First round - 2016 - Reconstruction of the Awakening Boulevard in the stage from Kalpaka bridge to Turaida street. Realization requires 360 thousand euros. Second round - in 2017 - Reconstruction of the Awakening Boulevard in the phase from Turaida Street to Piedeste Street. Realization requires 1 million EUR 200 000. In view of this, please support Liepaja city's prepared project for reconstruction of the Atmogas Boulevard, transferring 360 thousand euros from the state budget in 2016 for the first stage of its implementation. Thank you for your attention.

A very practical example: in Kārsava we also need a stadium, and Kārsava is a border town, a border region where it is important for us to ensure that people live there so that young people can exercise there, so that local border guards can also train there. Why can we not, after some logic, support these children's fields, sports grounds, and so on, and so on? ( The internouncing of the grass: “Because we are in opposition!” ) In essence, colleagues, as a deputy head of a political group, I should be asked to vote on each of the next proposals. There are 100 proposals. If I ask everyone for a vote, it will take a very long time. Do you really, fellow Members, want to vote in favour of every proposal of this kind, where there are one thousand, two thousand, as I said? Last year, I also came to this conference and I pointed to some of the brightest examples. I really was naively hoping that you would listen a little and that you would not put your local government or your friends' municipalities or their societies' projects in the state budget for this year, where there are more than 7 billion that we have to use in line with the priorities, in line with the Government Declaration, and so on, and so on. I do not want to read you morally, but, my fellow Members, do not do so next year, because then I will really be asking for a vote on every proposal, and then all this will take several more hours. ( Interfaces from the drug: “We will not be able to do it many times! ” ) And if you have a lot of jokes, gentlemen, and do not really want to take my words seriously, I will really ask for a vote on each of your next proposals. ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U1823	Dear colleagues, The Latvian National Development Plan, approved by the Saeima, identified education sector as one of the priorities for economic growth immediately behind competitiveness in entrepreneurship. Even though Prime Minister Laimdota Straujuma has come to the conclusion in March this year that next year's budget priority will be another, namely protection and security, the education sector remains at the top of the priorities right behind the health sector - fourth. Thank you to those who have stated that they should take care of the security of their citizens, but I call on everyone to answer the question: will it become safer in Latvia if young people go to study abroad and do not return if they work and contribute to the economy of other countries? I don't think so. In this situation, it is strange to see that one of the priority sectors, namely the Ministry of Education and Science, is reducing the allocated funding by almost 30 per cent compared to the previous year. It is convenient to operate with interest on gross domestic product because any decision does not exceed 5 per cent and does not cut in the ears. But why not look at the dramatic changes in the priority sectors? For the first two – the Ministry of Defence and the Ministry of the Interior – funding has increased by 45 percent and 11 percent respectively compared to the previous year. The Ministry of Health has retained funding at the level of the previous year, while the next priority sector - the Ministry of Education and Science - has a sharp reduction of 30 percent. It is interesting that money has also been found in the Ministry of Agriculture with an increase of 13 percent and in the Ministry of Justice - plus four percent. We have the lowest salaries for educators, the lowest funding for science and universities, as well as for interest education. I call on you to avoid further cuts in funding in the education sector and to support the increase in targeted grants for municipalities.

ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U1849	Dear colleagues and honourable colleagues. Let me start with a concrete example. There is an opportunity to implement the project of restoration of the part of Riga Street, which is mostly financed by the National Road Fund, thus from the state budget. The total amount of the project is less than EUR three million. Valka municipality should provide co-financing - almost 600 thousand euros. The municipality itself does not have such funds, but it could borrow them. But only 75 percent of the missing part will be able to borrow according to the draft budget law. The municipality of Valka will therefore have to bear 25 per cent of its own revenues. ( From the drug, ‘ Normal! ’) ) Very trenching... Well, what about... It's normal for us, for whom it's not quite... It may also get these missing 25 percent by re-allocating the budget, but it is not in principle correct in conceptual terms. If the State allocates EUR 2.4 million, the municipality should be given the opportunity to borrow the full amount of co-financing so that the project can be carried out at all. I have to give you an opportunity! Why do we believe here that we are bigger, smarter and more reasonable than the municipalities and that the municipalities are a kindergarten that needs to be enough, and that we need to identify the municipalities how they should deal with their own budget? I think we need to support the independence of the municipalities, not to attract more and more the hands of the municipalities. Because what if they can't afford these 25 percent from their income? But these are important national projects! Therefore, please support the proposal to eliminate this risk and solve the problem - to supplement Article 15, third paragraph, point 3, with the following third sentence: "The condition concerning the actual contribution of the municipal budget shall not apply to urban recovery projects co-financed by the state budget." And again the justification. In order to receive co-financing of state budget funds for urban transit street restoration projects, municipalities should ensure a certain part of their funding in accordance with the Cabinet Regulations of 11 March 2008 No.1973 “Procedure for the use of funds allocated to the National Road Fund programme of the State Budget. In 2016, municipalities will be able to borrow 75 percent of the necessary funding for road investment projects if you do not support my proposal. However, given the importance of the project for the restoration of urban transit streets, my proposal provides for the possibility for local authorities to borrow the full amount they have to provide as part of the project for the restoration of transit streets so that these projects can be carried out safely. I ask you to support my proposal.

ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U1877	Thank you, colleagues, because I would like to talk about proposals 19 and 24. In turn, the other two will be kept in semi-bath next year and will remain as if not even more as this year or last year. Contrary to pre-election promises about a substantial and gradual increase in expenditure on health care and the approximation of expenditure at least to the level of Lithuania and Estonia, reality is harsh. Power parties have forgotten their promises to the voters, and next year, the Ministry of Health's budget will have fallen below the 3 percent mark, i.e. 2.9 percent. I want to ask you: how long will this promised health expenditure "growth" (in feet) continue with a minus sign? I believe that, if we continue, the promised 4.5 percent of GDP in 2020 will turn into 2 percent or even one-and-a-half percent. You could already smile at "how you can't do it" if it wasn't so tragic. Tragically tens of thousands of Latvians who do not have money to get to the doctor, do not have money to carry out investigations, have no money to buy even the state partly paid medicine or paid operation, because the state paid has to stand in line for months and years. People are, of course, in the trap of everyone and everything related to health care: “We have the most expensive doctors, the most expensive surgery, the most expensive medicine!” However, I think that it is not all more expensive for us than in other countries; the difference is simply that in other countries most of these costs, such as the cost of medicines, are paid by the state. We're not even covered by half of the expenses. Or, for example, private insurance. We have to pay almost everything for the patient himself. In Europe, on average, public budgets finance healthcare at 75 per cent. In our country, it is somewhere about 50 percent. As a result, there were already more than 168 thousand people with disabilities in Latvia in 2014, it is almost 30 thousand more than in 2010, including 7924 children. This means that almost every tenth, I draw attention to - every tenth of Latvia's population has a certain disability. But the biggest concern is that every year some 16 thousand people's disability is being determined for the first time. Mostly in people with chronic conditions that could be treated. As the population of the country declines and the number of people with disabilities increases, it can be safely predicted that in 2017 disability will be determined more than for every tenth. There are human suffering, deterioration in quality of life, and restrictions behind these figures. There is also a wonderful loss of state because of the need for budgetary expenditure on benefits and pensions for people with disabilities, employers who will soon no longer have workers, which will cover the costs of the sick and the unemployed. Therefore, I invite you to support the 19th proposal of “From the heart to Latvia”: 95 percent of the excess tax revenue planned in the state budget in 2016 is directed towards health care; and 24th proposal: to instruct the Cabinet of Ministers to develop and submit to the Saeima a timetable for improving the availability and quality of health care by 30 August 2016, with an increase of state funding for health care of at least 5 percent of gross domestic product in 2020. Thank you.

However, every year, when drawing up the public budget, the government regrets that tax revenues are insufficient to cover all the necessary expenditure, and that there is a need to raise taxes again. In our view, it is important that the way in which funds are spent and what expenditure they are spent is known not only by an official but also by ministers and also by Members, and that society is able to obtain all the necessary information at any time when this is necessary. And therefore, in our view, it is not enough to implement this general rule, which is enshrined in the law, which obliges us to review budgetary expenditure, because, as we can see, the executive power of the norm contained in the Law on Budgets and Financial Management can quietly ignore and not in any way implement by submitting the annual budget law. It would therefore be important - and this is what this proposal is about - that there is a specific timetable, a task for the Cabinet of Ministers, over a period of time, to develop a methodology for reviewing the basic expenditure so that there is no need to discuss any arguments again, because there will not work on the argument that there was elections, there will not work on the argument that there was a Presidency. But I assume that officials will always find in their creation a way to find again something to excuse, why it is impossible to perform the works, tasks and functions that the law lays down. Therefore, I invite you to support our proposal, which is to entrust the Cabinet of Ministers with the preparation of the methodology for the evaluation of basic expenditure of the State budget programme and the budget evaluation schedule of ministries and other central state institutions for 2016. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U1914	Let us start a debate. Name to Member Igor Pimenov.
ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U1915	Good evening, honourable colleagues! I propose to submit to the Cabinet of Ministers a draft law on amendments to the Law “On Corporate Income Tax”, which ensures that, from the second half of next year, when determining the income subject to corporate income tax, it is reduced by a part of income that was not set for dividends, as well as by the income invested during the tax period. What is the profit invested? Briefly, it is a part of the company’s profits that is not paid out in dividends but is left to ensure the company’s economic activity by investing in the company’s assets. I also refer to undistributed profits in the draft law. Since 2004, the corporate tax rate has been 15 per cent. Latvia has 20 corporate tax incentives or tax incentives. Several of them can be applied at the same time, leading to an effective rate of 6.4 per cent. These are incentives for investment, incentives for sectors, incentives for social goals and others. The existing system of relief looks very coherent and effective. However, it is too complex and administratively costly. The non-taxing of reinvested profits through corporate income will provide a clear, visible incentive for the entrepreneur to behave, i.e. to invest profits in production rather than in consumption, and not to hide profits through various accounting procedures, reducing the tax payable. There is another argument in favour of the proposed model. Banks currently credit only large companies, which are also able to benefit from a wide range of tax incentives, while small and medium-sized enterprises, which are not credited by the banks, are forced to pay full corporate tax. So they will have even less money to invest in development. Non-taxation of reinvestable profits would solve the shortage of borrowed funds. Moreover, the possibility of not paying tax on undistributed profits would allow entrepreneurs not to borrow in banks, but to accumulate funds for large investments over 2-5 years. The non-taxation of undistributed profits and reinvested profits of an enterprise with corporate income tax will result in an increasing fiscal effect if it is applied at the same time as the introduction of progressive personal income tax. The businessman will be more motivated to invest profits in his own company's infrastructure, manufacturing, thus economic development, and the money earned will not be used for personal spending - new cars and entertainment - as high personal income tax makes the distribution of profits less harmful to dividends. Both taxes will also be effective on their own, but even more effective synergistic results will be achieved if they are applied at the same time. As far as I know, a working group on the development of progressive personal income tax will start working at the Ministry of Finance. I therefore propose that, together with this progressive tax, we also initiate a new reform process on corporate taxation. Let us remember that next to Estonia, this methodology works quite successfully! I think there is enough time until 1 March to allow us to assess our neighbours' experience. Norway shall apply a similar methodology. It seems to me that in our respectable country we can apply the reformed two taxes - in favour of Latvia's development, in favour of companies' success, in favour of greater revenue in our state budget. It seems to me that this is a good way forward, and I think that if we support my proposal, it will only benefit Latvia's economic development. I am now awaiting a comment from our President. Thank you.

It is the most difficult and expensive event for a person to change habits. He's used to listening to his radio station or his television channel. You can't buy his habits by 155 thousand. As a result, there will be a “Latvian Radio 5” event in a square. Because next year you'll need the money again. You create another cash hole in this way. And that's not right. You can't do that! Information is the most expensive on this planet, but content is the most expensive in the media environment. Here's the way that 155 thousand will be bought irons, flashlights, cameras, some computers and one little server, and next year you'll come and ask twice. And then three times! And it will be with those 2.3 million Latvian Radio 5 with a audience of 16 thousand. But this is about studying. They don't even know which city they're going to do it! Mjartan has never even looked at it. Is that Rēzekne? Will it be Daugavpils? There's no news at all. While in the Committee on Budgets and Finance (Tax) and seeing that the entire coalition abstained from my offer - to take away the budget of “Latvijas Radio 5” here, I realized that there is no sense at all about what media environment in Latvia. I call for this money not to be provided for the creation of a new multimedia studio in Latgale. This must be changed and there must be a public order. It is much more valuable and it will work more in the longer term. Do not create a new pit of money! Please vote against!

I believe in this, and I know that Latvia will move forward in the 98th year of existence, as in the years to come. With the joy of development and calm conviction. This is the budget of every Latvian citizen, on which you will be voting immediately. This budget makes every effort to ensure that, in the current economic climate, we balance the interests of different groups of society in order to reduce inequalities in the income of citizens. Additional resources have been invested in security and internal security. An additional 9 million are invested in education, an additional 10 million are directed towards health. Additional funds have been allocated to police and firemen. There have been regular constructive and emotional discussions with the cooperation and social partners. We can always teach ourselves a lesson for the future about the time, opportunities and results of dialogue. I believe that work on the state budget for 2017 should start already in December, involving the visions and ideas of the partners in each of the sectors. The government has been responsible in budgeting. Criticism has been both healthy, encouraging and self-targeted. However, I can assure you that we have been actively involved with you, the whole parliament, in the best possible way, taking into account the external challenges that Latvia will have to face in the coming years. And here I would like to thank our Minister for Finance, the Ministry of Finance, the Parliamentary Secretary of the Ministry of Finance and the Parliamentary Committee on Budgets and Finance (tax) and its Chairman, Kārlis Šadurskis, personally for two months' work after the transfer of the budget to the Saeima. ParlaMint-LV_2015-11-30-PT12-333-U2908	Dear colleagues, The fact that this budget is very, very poorly prepared, is probably understood by everyone, except by the ruling coalition. Teachers striked, entrepreneurs gave unusual harsh words to the performance of the Ministry of Finance and government, medical staff only at the last moment, received promises to do something for the benefit of the case, refused active protests. The recently published SKDS study shows that almost half of the respondents are considered officials to be the main beneficiaries of this budget, and a third to unscrupulous entrepreneurs. On the other hand, honest entrepreneurs and families with children are considered to be the greatest losers. There is something to think about for at least one coalition partner who is thinking about demographic issues in particular. Also, the rhetoric of the ruling coalition about reducing inequality has not found hearing ears. Only 5.9 percent of respondents believe that the benefit will be low income, only 2.5 percent believe that pensioners. It seems that one brother's work is also understood by the members of the ruling coalition, but it does not prevent them from voting in favour of this budget, because the increase in the monthly salaries of ministers and other officials, for example, is as high as the usual teacher who has not traditionally found the money for the rise in wages. We cannot support a tax policy that has changed literally on a day-to-day basis and that does not arrogantly listen to proposals based on either employers or trade unions, with a clear disregard for the views of the business community that generates tax revenues. The fact that the draft laws drawn up by the Cabinet of Ministers are of low quality and unthinking nature has been indirectly demonstrated by the Cabinet itself, which has submitted a whole amount - about 200 - for the improvement of draft laws and the improvement of its brother. The revenue policy proposed in the budget resembles a circle of happiness marked by all groups of taxpayers. Which of them will be stopped by the circle cut by the Cabinet of Ministers, they will pay more this year. Nothing to do, that's the fate! Instead of seriously combating the shadow economy, this year's budgetary policy focuses on the additional taxation of groups that already fairly pay taxes. It was only last Friday that a decision was made to waive the unlimited increase in the minimum, instead of increasing the facilities for the dependent person by ten euros. Does this really indicate a thoughtful, timely state policy? Nor is it acceptable for us to increase excise duty on fuel, which will have a significant impact on the prices of goods and services, and this revenue will not be earmarked for road repairs at all. The amendment of the value added tax, which provides for the taxation of property management services in multi-apartment buildings with value added tax, will also have a significant impact on people's chances of paying for housing, including not the richest ones themselves. It was only at the last moment that objections to the application of VAT to cultural events were heard when people in the public began to protest against them. Only 92 percent of SKDS respondents who consider auditing and saving public expenditure as a primary objective to increase the allocation of funds to areas of national importance can be agreed. Unfortunately, this has proved to be a difficult task that has been put on the weak shoulders of the government. We cannot support part of the expenditure, not only because it is impossible to understand what services we receive and to what extent, but also because the Cabinet of Ministers does not have consistency, it changes quite dynamically, and suddenly there are various additional means to meet certain requirements and thus preserve the stability of the coalition. In fact, this year, too, contrary to what has been promised, Members' quotas have been maintained, allowing money to be shared without clear criteria for all sorts of small and potential voters' needs. It is a feeling that dozens of Christmas grandparents or rather shawls and snow-whites, who call themselves Members, have taken the low start to bring gifts bought for taxpayers' money to Latvia very soon. A kindergarten will have a playground, a sports complex, but a school will only have to accept an interactive board. Sometimes there is a feeling that the country has enough money and that there is no shortage of the necessary addressees to cover it. In recent days both the increase in wages and salaries of the Cabinet of Ministers and the study of the World Bank on the tax system have been found. With the adoption of this budget, there is still uncertainty about the overlapping of airBaltic with the EUR 80 million, about the provision of assistance to Turkey and, in the strongest sense, about what the real potential of our national budget is, what the objectives of this government are and what it wants to achieve with this budget. Therefore, the “From the heart to Latvia” group cannot support the 2016 state budget and believes that it will not contribute to Latvia’s goals, it will not contribute to Latvia’s growth, but it will have a negative impact on the business environment and citizens’ well-being. (Applause)
